AI時代を読み解く⑦ 
投資循環と市場予想から考える 
AIブームはいつまで続き、どんな帰結を迎えるか

○ 企業がすでに持っている設備の増え方と、新しく行っている投資の勢いを組み合わせた絵を描く
ことで、投資ブームがどの段階にあるのかがわかる。これはストック循環図と呼ばれている 
○ ITブームのストック循環図と重ねてみると、AIブームはまだ入り口に過ぎない。今後、期待成長
率やストックに対する投資の大きさがどう変化するのかが、AIブームの持続性を占うカギになる 
○ AIブームの帰結を占う上ではアナリスト予想が役に立つ。需要側と供給側に分けてそれぞれのシ
ナリオを考えると「深刻な分配問題」や「金融安定問題」が浮かび上がる

１．ストック循環図が示す現在地 
企業がすでに持っている設備の増え方と、新しく行っている投資の勢いを組み合わせた絵を描くこ
とで、投資ブームがどの段階にあるのかがわかる。これはストック循環図と呼ばれている。 
ストック循環図を作るのは簡単で、横 軸 に1期前の投資・ストック比率、 縦軸に設備投資の伸び率を
とってプロットする。また、観察期間を通じて得られる2つのパラメーター（資本係数1の伸びと資本
減耗率）を一定とすると、投資活動に織り込まれた投資主体の期待成長率を表わす曲線を描くことが
できる。期待成長率が高いほど、成長率曲線は右側に描かれる。地図でいえば経度のようなもので、
ストック循環図のプロット点がどの成長率曲線上にあるのかをみれば、「成長率はまだ低い。投資は
まだ続く」「成長率は高すぎる。投資はそろそろピークが近い」といった判断が可能になる。 
期待成長率が大きく変わらない小規模な景気循環であれば、 ストック循環図は1本の成長率曲線を軸
にした軌道上を時計回りに動く（図表
1）。一方、数年にわたって続く投資ブ
ームとその終焉というケースでは、ス
トック循環図は大きく横に 膨らんだ形
になる。 ブームが続いている間は、 設備
投資の高い伸びと投資・ストック比率
の上昇によって、ストック循環図は右
へとドリフトしていく。ちょうど、 より
高い期待成長率をあらわす成長率曲線
を目指す動きになるわけだ。 しかし期
待成長率の大幅な下方修正を 強いるよ
うなショックが起きると 、設備投資の
伸びがマイナスになり、ストック循環

### Page 2

2

図のプロット点は急降下する。そして、数年かけてもとの成長率曲線かさらに低い成長率曲線が示す
場所へと戻っていく（左へのドリフト）。 
２．AI ブームはまだ入り口 
急速かつ巨額の投資が相次ぐAIブームだが、ITブームのストック循環図と重ねてみると、AIブーム
はまだ入り口に過ぎない。 今後、 期待成長率やストックに対する投資の大きさがどう変化するのかが、
AIブームの持続性を占うカギになる。 
図表2は、2019年以降のストック循環図を描いたものでIT投資に焦点を当てている。1990年代のITブ
ーム期におけるストック循環図2と重ねてみると、2026年のプロット点は1995年のプロット点とほぼ一
致することがわかる。また成長率曲線をみてみると、2026年は10％であり、1996年当時と同じ高さで
ある（成長率曲線のパラメーターが異なるため、同じ期待成長率でも、現下の成長率曲線とITブーム
の成長率曲線は一致しない）。 
現下のAIブームが始まったのは2023年ごろであり、4年目に突入している。 しかし、ITブームのスト
ック循環図を「地図」に見立てるなら、まだ入り口に過ぎず、AI関連のIT投資にはまだ伸び余地が残
っているということだ。横軸の投資・ストック比率は2026年時点で20％台後半であり、ITブームが終
盤を迎えた頃の40％にはかなりの距離がある。ストック対比でみて過剰な投資が行われているという
わけではないのである。 
AIブームがいつまで続くのか。設備投資の伸び率とストック対比でみた投資規模、そしてそれらに
織り込まれた投資主体の期待成長率の推移に注目していくことによって、その持続性を推し量ること
ができる。

### Page 3

3

図表2からは、AIブームが今後も数年にわたって続く余地が示唆される。1990年代のITブームと同様
のペースで進むと仮定すれば、あと5年程度が一つの目安となる。 
もっとも、供給を上回る投資需要が強いインフレ圧力を生んでいることや、（中間選挙年というこ
ともあり）データセンター投資が政治問題化していることなど、ITブームには見られなかった障害・
摩擦が生まれていることも確かだ。こうした問題が、AIブームの持続性を左右しかねない点には注意
が必要である。 
３．アナリスト予想から導き出される AI ブームの政策的課題 
ストック循環図からみれば、AIブームには今後も数年にわたって続く余地がある。では、その先に
何が待っているのか。この点を考える上で手掛かりとなるのが、アナリストによる業績予想である。
AIブームを需要側と供給側に分けて将来像を描いてみると、すでに多くの識者が警鐘を鳴らしている
「深刻な分配問題」と「金融安定問題」が浮かび上がってくる。 
まず需要側の手掛かりとして、ハイパースケーラーの営業キャッシュフローに注目した。大規模な
データセンターを抱えるハイパースケーラーは、最終的にどのAIモデルが市場を制するとしても、AI
の利用そのものが広がれば恩恵を受ける立場にある。もちろん、彼らの営業キャッシュフローにはAI
以外の事業も含まれる。しかし、その将来の伸びが主としてAI関連事業によって左右されるのであれ
ば、アナリストが予想する営業キャッシュフローの増加は、AIの実装拡大によって生み出される需要
の大きさを測る一つの代理変数とみなすことができる。 
これに対して、供給側の手掛かりとなるのがGPU最大手の売上高である。さらに、過去のGPU売上高
と計算資源量との関係を利用すれば、アナリストによる売上高予想を、将来供給される計算資源量へ
と読み替えることができる。 
ここでいう計算資源量は、単純なGPUの台数ではない。GPUは世代や機種によって演算性能が大きく
異なるため、異なるチップをそのまま足し合わせても経済的な意味は乏しい。そこで、それぞれのチ
ップの演算性能を基準となるGPU（モデルGPU3）の性能に換算し、「モデルGPU何台分の計算能力に相
当するか」で集計する。これは、品質調整された物価指数を用いて名目設備投資を実質設備投資に変
換するのと似ている。GPUという物理的な台数ではなく、 性能差を調整した 「実質的な計算能力のスト
ック」を捉える考え方である。 
このようにGPU売上高の予想を計算資源量に変換することで、AIブームが今後どれだけの計算能力を
経済の中に積み上げていくのかを推計できる。需要側ではハイパースケーラーの営業キャッシュフロ
ー、 供給側ではGPU売上高とそこから導かれる計算資源量を見ることで、AIブームの将来像を金額と物
量の両面から捉えることができる。 
図表3は、2030年のハイパースケーラーの営業キャッシュフローとGPU最大手の売上高に関するアナ
リスト予想と、後者を計算資源量に変換したものである。 
ハイパースケーラーの営業キャッシュフローは、2030年時点で2兆ドルを超えると予想されており、
2025年対比では3.4倍（平均年率＋27%）に達する。一方、GPU最大手の売上高は1兆144億ドルと予想さ
れており、こちらも2025年対比4.9倍（平均年率＋37％）と大きな伸びとなっている。さらにこれをモ
デルGPUで測った計算資源量に換算すると、2030年の計算資源量は2025年の13倍から36倍となる（幅を
もたせたのは、売上高と計算資源量の関係を計算するためのデータ数が少ないため。前 者 は線形関係、

### Page 4

4

後者はべき乗関係を想定したものである）。 
ここで悩ましいのは、こ の2つの予想を同時に成立させるのが難しいことである。 計算資源量が13倍
から36倍になる一方、 需要の代理変数であるキャッシュフローは3.4倍にとどまる。 果たしてこれは両
立するのか。 
まず、 供給サイドの予測が正しく、 かつGPUの収益性が不変だとすれば、ハイパースケーラーの営業
キャッシュフローの予測が過小評価されていることになる。アナリスト予想にすら織り込まれていな
い 「知られざる巨大な需要」 ――概算で8兆ドルから22兆ドル――がなければならない。 しかし、これ
だけの市場がすべて新たに生まれると考えるのはかなり大胆な想定であり、むしろ、既存の市場やプ
レーヤーから付加価値を奪うということではないだろうか。つまり、 供給サイドの予測が正しければ、
企業間格差や企業・資本家と労働者の格差が拡大し、「深刻な分配問題」が生じるシナリオが導かれ
るのである。 
次に、需要サイドの予測が正しければ何が起きるのか。容易に想像できるように、それは計算資源
当たりの収益性が大幅に下落するシナリオであり、AIエコシステムを支える企業群のバリュエーショ
ンは言うまでもなく、ベンダーや顧客への出資や保証、自社製品の再販価値保証といった「AI時代の
金融イノベーション」の経済的合理性が問われることになる。収益性の低下や資産価値の下落が、こ
うした出資・保証関係を通じて波及すれば、「金融安定問題」につながり得る。 
ITブームに比べると、AIブームはプレーヤーが少ない分だけモニタリングが容易である。主要AIラ
ボがIPOに成功すれば、新たな情報が提供されるだろう。 本稿の思考実験は、 追加情報を整理していく
うえでも役に立つはずである。

1 資本係数＝IT 機器の実質ストック÷実質GDP。 
2 ストック循環図は実質値で作成するが、横軸の投資・ストック比率は、実質系列（連鎖式）の基準年から離れるほど経済的な
意味をもたなくなる。そのため、現在の公表値を用いて IT ブームのストック循環図を作ることはできず、当時のビンテージデ
ータを用いる必要がある。本稿で用いたのは 1996 年基準である。 
3 NVIDIA 社の H100 換算。Epoch AI による換算値。

• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。

美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

獲利韌性與金庫深度：強勁EBITDA成為信用安全網
• 上兆美元的流動性護城河： 儘管債務增加，但科技十二巨頭合計持有近 1兆美元的等值現金，整體科技板塊流動性充裕 ，短
期內無流動性危機。
• EBITDA強勁成長消化債務：市場預期覆蓋的12家最大科技企業在2026與2027年的EBITDA合計將成長約35%，強勁的獲利
能力顯著降低槓桿比率的上升壓力，確保信評機構維護穩定的信用評級。
• 資本配置策略分化：(1)兼顧股東回報：蘋果、微軟、Meta與Alphabet在投入AI同時，仍維持大規模的庫藏股買回與股息發
放。(2)全數投入資本支出：亞馬遜將絕大部分現金導向資本支出(2026年資本支出預計達2,200億美元居首)，且不發放股息。
• 小結與投資建議：科技巨頭為維持AI技術與基礎設施領先優勢，短期內，負自由現金流將成為常態。然而，這並非財務危機

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.09。

——8 月经济数据解读
 8 月工业增加值当月同比 5.20%，预期 4.70%，前值 4.50%；8 月工业增加值
累计同比 5.30%，前值 5.30%；8 月固定资产投资完成额累计同比-7.20%，预

期-7.20%，前值-6.70%；8月社会消费品零售总额当月同比0.40%，预期0.70%，
前值 0.60%；8 月社会消费品零售总额累计同比 1.10%，前值 1.20%。我们解
读如下：
 一、国际油价上破 150美元/桶概率较低，明年 2 季度之后美联储加息概率不
大；
 二、8 月工业增加值数据总体表现尚可，但年内工业增加值当月同比或承压；
 三、房地产投资增速短期还将继续下探，房价同比“转正”时点是企稳“观
察锚”之一；
 四、城镇调查失业率上行，叠加美联储加息及 M2同比走弱，我国消费增速或
还将承压。
 风险提示：1.美伊战争持续时长超预期；2.美国通胀上行幅度高于预期。
 注：本文所有数据，均来自 WIND资讯，经英大证券整理。

### Page 3

宏观研究报告
请务必阅读最后一页的免责条款 3
事件：8 月工业增加值当月同比 5.20%，预期 4.70%，前值 4.50%；8 月工业增加值
累计同比 5.30%，前值 5.30%；8 月固定资产投资完成额累计同比-7.20%，预期-7.20%，
前值-6.70%；8月社会消费品零售总额当月同比 0.40%，预期 0.70%，前值 0.60%；8 月社
会消费品零售总额累计同比 1.10%，前值 1.20%。
解读：
一、国际油价上破 150美元/桶概率较低，明年 2季度之后美联储加息概率不大
国际油价剧烈波动是 2026年全球宏观经济面临的不确定性之一。当前国际油价位于
90-100美元/桶之间，主要支撑因素是美伊战争对原油供给的冲击，以及美国宏观经济在
2026年走强。在这两个因素的作用下，短期内国际油价还将位于一定高位。
当下资本市场关注的焦点之一是，本轮周期国际油价能否突破 150美元/桶。我们认
为国际油价突破 150美元/桶的概率较低。我们做出这一判断的依据来自需求与供给两个

方面。从需求端看：一是由于原油消费的刚性，短期之内国际油价上涨推升消费增速，
但是随着时间的推移，高油价对消费群体的收入形成较大压力，进而在中期反噬全球对
原油的需求，对国际油价形成利空；二是在 9 月美联储启动加息之后，后续还将继续加
息，而在加息之前美国联邦基金目标利率就已经位于相对高位，“高位加息”进一步推
升美国联邦基金目标利率，压制全球宏观经济，进而对国际油价形成利空。从供给端看：
一是波斯湾地区之外的原油生产，并未因为美伊战争封锁波斯湾而停止，相反，受全球
供给短期短缺以及连带的高油价的刺激，产油国大概率加大生产力度，以填补伊朗原油
缺位留下的全球供给缺口，这一点可以得到数据证实：4-8月，委内瑞拉原油月均产量在
100万桶的基础上不断上行，创 2019年 2 月以来新高，与此同时，美国原油月均产量持
续位于 1350万桶之上，且总体呈上行走势；二是随着时间的推移，海湾地区原油运输基
础设施不断建设与完善，体现在沙特阿拉伯的原油产量上：3月受美伊战争的影响，沙特
阿拉伯的原油产量从 2 月的 1011.2 万桶，快速下跌至 762.6万桶，在 4 月跌至 675.5 万

桶之后，总体呈上行走势，8 月录得 727.6 万桶。也就是说，随着时间的推移，伊朗对波
斯湾的封锁效应，以及连带的对国际油价的推升力度，大概率是减弱的。
图 1：委内瑞拉原油月均产量在 100 万桶的基础上不断上行（万桶）
数据来源：Wind资讯，英大证券整理
综上，我们认为在需求端中期边际收缩以及供给端中短期边际扩张的背景下，国际
油价在 2026 年 9 月至 2027年 12 月不具备冲击 150 美元/桶的潜力。鉴于国际油价走势

宏观研究报告
请务必阅读最后一页的免责条款 4
是美国 CPI同比的主导因素，因此在国际油价难以大幅冲高的假设下，我们认为 2027年
3月美国 CPI同比大概率承压下行，这将降低美联储加息的概率，增加美联储维持利率不
变的概率，并加大市场的降息预期。值得指出的是，根据历史经验，除非特殊情况，美
联储不会在加息结束后快速启动降息进程，叠加利率高位对美国宏观经济的压制效应需
要一段时间才能体现，尤其是美国 CPI同比可能会有一段时间的反复，因此美联储下一
次降息的时间点，可能位于 2027年 4季度或 4季度之后。
二、8 月工业增加值数据总体表现尚可，但年内工业增加值当月同比或承压
8月工业增加值数据总体表现尚可：一是 8月工业增加值当月同比录得 5.20%，高于
预期 0.50 个百分点，较前值大幅上行 0.70 个百分点；二是 8 月工业增加值累计同比录
得 5.30%，持平于前值，为 2024年 1 月以来低值；三是 8月工业增加值环比录得 0.54%，
较前值上行 0.43个百分点，创近 3 年同期新高。

分部类看，8 月采矿业、制造业与电力燃气及水的生产和供应业工业增加值当月同比
分别为-1.40%、6.10%、4.90%，分别较前值上行 2.80、0.60、-0.10 个百分点。其中：8
月制造业工业增加值当月同比较前值大幅上行0.60个百分点，创2025年10月以来新高，
是 8 月工业增加值当月同比的主要支撑项；8 月采矿业工业增加值当月同比虽然大幅上行
2.80 个百分点，是绝对读数依旧位于负值区间，且处于历史低位区间；8 月电力燃气及
水的生产和供应业工业增加值当月同比虽然较前值小幅下行，但绝对读数位于相对高位。
新动能方面，8月高技术产业工业增加值当月同比录得 16.70%，虽然较前值下行 0.20
个百分点，但是连续 7个月录得两位数增长，且为 2021年 9月以来次高值。与高技术产
业工业增加值当月同比相关联的一个指标是高技术制造业 PMI。从图形走势看，高技术制
造业 PMI 领先高技术产业工业增加值当月同比：高技术产业工业增加值当月同比的三个
低点分别是 2019 年 8 月的 6.10%、2020 年 10 月的 6.30%，以及 2023 年 7 月的 0.70%，

与之相对应，高技术制造业 PMI的三个低点分别是 2018年 12月的 48.10%、2020年 6 月
的 51.10，以及 2022年 12 月的 47.40，也就是说，高技术制造业 PMI 的三个低点分别领
先高技术产业工业增加值当月同比的三个低点 8 个月、4个月、7个月。
图 2：高技术制造业 PMI领先高技术产业工业增加值当月同比（%；%）
数据来源：Wind资讯，英大证券整理
在美联储加息的情况下，高技术制造业 PMI 是承压下行的，而在美联储降息的背景
下，高技术制造业 PMI 是上行的。当前美联储在高位加息，则高技术制造业 PMI大概率

宏观研究报告
请务必阅读最后一页的免责条款 5
会面临压力，进而对高技术产业工业增加值当月同比形成一定压力。当然，对高技术产
业工业增加值也面临利多。9月 26 日中美两国元首就中美建设性战略稳定关系及重大国
际地区问题深入交换意见，达成八点成果共识。其中，第七项与高技术产业直接相关，
即“双方同意建立中美人工智能对话，交流人工智能相关风险和惠益。下次对话将于今
年 11 月举行。双方同意建立人工智能事件的沟通渠道”，此外，第五项与高技术产业间
接相关，即“两国元首认可中美经贸磋商机制积极作用和双方经贸团队磋商成果，包括
建立并推进贸易理事会等机制，达成‘300亿美元’对等降税安排，延期吉隆坡经贸磋商
成果等，并指示予以落实。”值得指出的是，虽然高技术产业因为中美两国元首会晤面临
产业角度的利多，但是美联储加息使得高技术产业面临来自宏观角度的利空。我们认为，
宏观角度的利空可能大于产业角度的利多。因此，我们认为高技术制造业增加值当月同
比的上行速度，可能会有一定程度的放缓。在此背景下，叠加国内需求依旧疲弱，使得

年内工业增加值当月同比或承压。
三、房地产投资增速短期还将继续下探，企稳时点或可观察房价同比“转正”时点
8月房地产开发投资完成额累计同比为-19.90%，较前值下行 0.70个百分点，创 1999
年 2 月有数据记录以来新低，持续 49个月位于负值区间。
仅从销售端看，8 月商品房销售面积累计同比为-12.10%，较前值下行 0.30 个百分点，
持续 4 个月下探，表明 5 月至 8月商品房销售又出现一定的走弱。与此同时，8 月商品房
销售额累计同比录得-13.00%，连续 6 个月单边累计上行 7.20 个百分点，创年内新高，
但是要注意到，2026 年 1-8 月的整体情况，弱于 2025年全年的-12.60%。最后，8 月商
品房待售面积累计同比录得-1.10%，较前值下行 0.30个百分点，连续 6 个月位于负值区
间，创 2021年 6月以来新低，表明商品房的库存情况持续好转。综上，虽然面临商品房
待售面积累计同比进入负值区间的利多，但是商品房销售面积累计同比在负值区间深度

下探，预示房地产开发投资完成额累计同比在短期内还将继续下探。
以上判断得到来自居民端的佐证。从当月值的角度看，8 月居民户新增中长期贷款为
-822.00亿元，环比少减 380.00 亿元，同比多减 1,022.00亿元；从累计值的角度看，1-8
月居民户新增中长期贷款为 188.00 亿元。不论是当月值，还是累计值，均创有数据记录
以来的“新低”：8月-822.00亿元的读数，是 2007年-2026年同期的最低值，且是唯一
一个进入负值区间的月份；1-8月 188.00 亿元的读数，是 2007年-2026 年同期的最低值。
以上表明当前我国居民对房贷的需求较为疲弱。这一点也得到资金端的印证：房地产开
发资金来源中的个人按揭贷款累计同比在 8 月录得-22.40%，虽然较前值上行 1.10 个百
分点，但是连续 35个月位于负值区间。
目前的问题是，在房地产投资增速大概率还将继续承压的背景下，房价增速能否起
来？客观而言，在绝大多数时间里，房地产开发投资完成额累计同比与 70 大中城市二手

住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比是正相关的，且 70 大中城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数
当月同比领先房地产开发投资完成额累计同比，但是自 2024 年 10 月起，二者走势发生
了背离：70大中城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比在负值区间持续上行，但是房
地产开发投资完成额累计同比在负值区间持续下探。对这一现象的一个解释是，虽然 70
大中城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比上行，但是依旧位于负值区间，所以不能
有效带动房地产开发投资完成额累计同比企稳上行。一个假设是，如果 70 大中城市二手
住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比上行至正值区间，则房地产开发投资完成额累计同比有
企稳上行的可能。

接下来的问题是，70大中城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比有无可能升至正
值区间？8 月该值的读数为-5.10%，创近 30 个月新高。从内部结构看，8 月一线城市、
二线城市、三线城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比分别录得-2.70%、-4.90%、
-5.60%，分别较前值上行 1.0、0.20、0.20 个百分点。其中，一线城市的读数最高，且
向“零值”趋近。按照这个趋势，一线城市的房价同比有可能“转正”。在一线城市房价
同比领先二线城市与三线城市的背景下，二线城市房价同比存在转正的可能。一旦二线
城市房价同比“转正”，则 70 大中城市二手住宅房屋销售价格指数当月同比大概率同时
“转正”，届时我们再观察房地产开发投资完成额累计同比是否能企稳。
四、城镇调查失业率上行，叠加美联储加息及 M2同比走弱，我国消费增速或还将承

压
8月主要失业率指标均不低于 7 月，表明 8月就业情况较 7 月出现边际走弱。具体看：
8 月城镇调查失业率录得 5.30%，较前值上行 0.10 个百分点，创近 5 个月新高；8 月 31
个大城市城镇调查失业率录得 5.30%，较前值上行 0.10个百分点，持平于 2026 年 3 月与
2025年8月，为2024年9月以来高点；8月本地户籍人口就业人员调查失业率录得5.30%，
较前值上行 0.10个百分点，创近 4 个月新高，为近 5 个月高点；8 月外来户籍人口就业
人员调查失业率录得 5.20%，持平于前值，为近 5 个月高点；8 月外来农业户籍人口就业
人员调查失业率录得 5.00%，较前值上行 0.10个百分点，创近 4 个月新高，为近 5 个月
高点。
分年龄组看，不含在校生的 8月 16-24岁劳动力、25-29岁劳动力、30-59 岁劳动力
城镇调查失业率分别录得 18.90%、7.50%、3.90%，分别较前值上行 1.0、0.30、0 个百分
点，其中，16-24岁劳动力城镇调查失业率持平于 2025年 8 月，为 2023年 12 月有数据

记录以来高点，25-29 岁劳动力城镇调查失业率创近 5 个月新高，而 30-59 岁劳动力城镇
调查失业率则处于历史低位区间。可以看出，初级劳动力面临更大的就业压力。
8月企业就业人员平均工作时间录得 48.20小时/周，连续 4个月持平于该值，为近
7个月高点。在城镇调查失业率系统性上升的背景下，企业就业人员平均工作时间依旧持
平于前值，表明在劳动力内部就业情况是冷热不均的：一部分人的工作时间上升，而另
一部分人的工作时间下降。从分年龄组城镇调查失业率的情况看，工作时间随着年龄的

增长而增加。这一现象对我国消费增速形成压力：相比 30-59 岁劳动力，16-24 岁劳动力
的工资收入低，且年轻人消费需求较为旺盛，边际消费倾向相对更高，也就是说，16-24

与 8 月城镇调查失业率系统性上升相对应，8 月消费数据系统性走弱。具体看：一是
8月社会消费品零售总额当月同比 0.40%，低于预期 0.30个百分点，较前值下行 0.20个
百分点，创近 4 个月新低；二是 8 月社会消费品零售总额累计同比 1.10%，较前值下行

我们认为，年内消费增速大概率还将承压。这一判断的依据，除了城镇调查失业率
上升之外，还来自两个方面。第一，年内美联储大概率至少再加息一次，这将对我国出
口增速形成一定程度的压制，尽管出口增速未必下行，进而对我国消费增速形成一定压

力，而从历史经验看，美联储加息利空我国宏观经济，进而利空我国消费增速。第二，8
月我国 M2 同比录得 7.50%，较前值下行 0.20 个百分点，创 2025年 4 月以来新低。这将
在一定程度上对我国消费增速形成压制。

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

4 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

and reuse, efficient computing, and

these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

August 2026: A slowdown of consumer price
inflation …
The average CPI inflation rate across the fifteen leading emerging economies stabilised at 4.3% year-on-year in August. The

inflationary impact remains weaker than in 2022, due in particular to reduced spillover effects on agricultural and food
prices. Manufacturers’ views on the trend in input and finished product prices have returned to their pre-conflict levels.
However, against a backdrop of increasingly frequent and destructive extreme weather events, pressure on agricultural and
food prices is likely to continue.
The manufacturing PMI improved very slightly in August but remains below its February 2026 level. The recovery remains

sluggish but is less volatile than in 2022.
According to estimates by the Institute for International Finance (IIF), non-resident portfolio investment flows into
emerging markets have been particularly volatile since the start of the armed conflict, reflecting investor nervousness.
Looking at cumulative figures since February provides a clearer picture of the trends. In the equity markets, the significant
outflows seen up to July (particularly in South Korea and Taiwan) came to a halt in August. Inflows into local bond markets

remained positive, although they slowed in August (averaging around USD 30 billion per month since April) Domestic
interest rates and risk premiums have performed very well.

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

and a hawkish Fed. Instead, the Swiss franc’s momentum was

Our colleagues analyse today’s budget announcement in France in
this note. ING’s view is that this budget buys some time but fails to
solve structural deﬁcit issues. Markets still have little incentive to
price ﬁscal premia out of OATs, and with no party having presented
a suﬃciently detailed spending cut plan, the upcoming elections

are adding to the uncertainty. Our rates team believes 150bp may
be the next step for the 10yr OAT-Bund spread.
That creates material downside risks for the euro. Rising yield
volatility and the prospect of wider spreads are pushing the market
back into an environment where the euro becomes much more
sensitive to the ﬁscal and bond narrative. We have seen similar
episodes before, often centred on Italy. Yet the euro's response has
so far been remarkably muted compared with those earlier periods.

In the chart below, we highlight (orange bars) episodes when the
average 10-year spread of France, Spain and Italy versus Germany
widened by at least four standard deviations relative to its six-
month mean. The light blue line shows the deviation of EUR/USD
from its short-term fair value, i.e. the risk premium on the euro. In
most of the other instances of sharp spread-widening, the euro
suﬀered from much more idiosyncratic pressure. For now, that

undervaluation is at a modest 1%.
The euro's reaction has been contained
compared to past episodes

### Page 3

EUR downside risks increasing
That is mostly bad news for EUR/USD, with a couple of caveats. The
negative aspect is that there is still plenty of room to catch up with
the ﬁscal story. The chart above suggests a 3% risk premium
buildup on the euro would be entirely in line with previous history,
meaning a move to 1.110 is a possibility in a scenario of further
spread widening, even if not extreme.
The positive caveat is that the limited EUR reaction may suggest

that markets are not as concerned as they were about the Italian
ﬁscal trajectory. The US is admittedly facing its own deal of ﬁscal
scrutiny on top of that, and part of the French spread widening has
surely followed higher energy prices, which as we know can fall
quite rapidly in an improved geopolitical landscape.
EUR/CHF is historically the cleanest way to hedge the eurozone
ﬁscal risk, and we could see further downside there too. Still, the
franc’s idiosyncratic fragilities may get in the way, meaning we

don’t expect CHF to outperform USD materially purely on the back
of the French story. A drop to 1.110 in EUR/USD could equal a move
to roughly 0.920-0.925 in EUR/CHF.

Document 284: OpenAI孵化的AI制药独角兽，9个月征服6家跨国药企，捅破行业天花板

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI孵化的AI制药独角兽，9个月征服6家跨国药企，捅破行业天花板.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 制药初创公司 Chai Discovery 成⽴仅两年多，九个⽉内已与礼来、辉瑞、诺华、 BMS 、
GSK 等六家药企达成合作，最新估值达 38 亿美元。与传统 AI 制药公司⾃建管线不同， Chai
专注模型授权和定制开发，凭借实验验证的设计能⼒获得药企认可。 Chai 最早是在
OpenAI 的办公室⾥孵化出来的， OpenAI ⾄今也是它的投资⼈之⼀。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 9 ⽇， AI 制药初创公司 Chai Discovery 官宣与 GSK （葛兰素史克）达成合作。
这看似是⼀笔常规跨国药企的 AI 合作，但值得关注的是，这已经是 Chai 九个⽉内签下的第六家
制药合作伙伴。
礼来、辉瑞、诺华、 argenx 、 BMS ，再加上 GSK ， Chai 已经拿下全球近半数 TOP10 药企。
⽽与它们合作的 Chai ，是⼀家 2024 年才成⽴，到今天还不满三岁的公司。
今年 7 ⽉， Chai Discovery 宣布完成 4 亿美元 C 轮融资，估值达到 38 亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 257 亿

元）。
更有意思的是，Chai ⼿⾥没有⼀条⾃研管线，也没有⼀个分⼦进⼊临床，它对外售出的只有⼀
样东西：模型。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻓期以来，向药企销售软件⼀直被视为天花板极低的业务，营收增⻓有限，但 Chai 似乎打破了
这个看法。
⼀家只卖模型的公司，凭什么让⼤药企排着队签约？
九个⽉，六张 MNC ⻔票
今年年初， Chai 联合创始⼈兼 CEO Josh Meier 曾公开表⽰， 2026 年将是 Chai 的 “ 部署之年 ” 。
梳理合作节奏，可以看出合作曲线很陡峭。从礼来到辉瑞， Chai ⽤了将近五个⽉；⽽从辉瑞到
GSK ，短短四个⽉⾥⼜连签了五家。
不仅如此，礼来在正式签约之前，还先拿 Chai 的⼀批模型设计做了内部评估。
⽽ GSK 也⾮常谨慎，从将信将疑到正式签约，整整花了⼀年。
2025 年 9 ⽉， GSK 研发技术⾼级副总裁、前美国国⽴卫⽣研究院（ NIH ）资深研究负责⼈
Christopher Austin 看完 Chai-2 的论⽂，就和 Chai 管理层⻅了⼀⾯。
他表⽰，那篇论⽂⾮常惊艳，但 “ 我们当然不相信 ” 。
双⽅此后⼀直保持联系，今年 1 ⽉在摩根⼤通医疗健康⼤会上再次碰⾯，敲定由 GSK 对 Chai 的技

术做⼀次独⽴测试， 2 ⽉正式启动。
靶点是 GSK ⾃⼰挑的，都是它感兴趣、也认为 Chai 模型有能⼒处理的项⽬； Chai 只负责交出零
样本⽣成的蛋⽩质序列，合成和实验验证全部在 GSK ⾃⼰的实验室⾥完成。
结果让 GSK 团队印象深刻，其内部湿实验在测试的所有靶点上都找到了能够结合的分⼦设计，
并且都是零样本⽣成的。尤其是有效抗体出现的频率和结合亲和⼒的强度。
Austin 表⽰： “ 我已经学会对这类事情保持怀疑，但这次的结果超出了我的预期。 ”
这位研发负责⼈也直⾔，他们考察了所有做 AI 蛋⽩设计的公司，并且⾮常挑剔。这意味着，⼤药
企愿意掏钱的，从来只是当下最强的那⼀两家。

### Page 3

最终双⽅敲定了正式合作。值得注意的是， Chai 和药企的合作的形式也在悄悄升级。
礼来是 “ 共建专属模型 ” ，辉瑞是 “ 授权使⽤新模型 ” ，到了 BMS ，已经变成把 Chai 的模型整合进抗
体发现管线。
早在今年 6 ⽉， Chai 就透露正在和超过 15 家药企谈判，并计划年内签下更多合作。
GSK ，⼤概率不会是最后⼀家。
只做软件不做管线
Chai Discovery 成⽴于 2024 年 3 ⽉，总部位于美国加利福尼亚州旧⾦⼭，是⼀家致⼒于使⽤ AI 赋
能药物开发的公司。
四位联合创始⼈ Joshua Meier 、 Jack Dent 、 Matthew McPartlon 和 Jacques Boitreaud ，来⾃
OpenAI 、 Meta FAIR 、 Stripe 和 Absci ，是典型的 “AI 原⽣ ” 组合。
公司最早甚⾄是在 OpenAI 的办公室⾥孵化出来的， OpenAI ⾄今也是它的投资⼈之⼀。
成⽴后不久，他们推出了第⼀代结构预测模型 Chai-1 ，在业内打响了名号。但真正让 Chai 从⼀

众 AI 制药公司⾥跳出来的，是 2025 年 6 ⽉发布的 Chai-2 。
按照 Chai 公布的数据， Chai-2 在 52 个全新靶点上做抗体从头设计，每个靶点只测试不超过 20 个
设计，就为⼤约⼀半的靶点找到了结合物，整体命中率在 16% 左右。
今年，它⼜推出了第三代模型 Chai-3 ，据称在⽣成符合治疗标准的抗体上，成功率是上⼀代的
两倍。辉瑞最终拍板签约，看中的正是 Chai-3 的设计能⼒。
过去⼗年， AI 制药公司⾛的⼤多是同⼀条路：⽤ AI 发现分⼦，⾃⼰推进管线，最后靠临床数据和
授权交易兑现价值。
Chai 选了⼀条截然相反的路，核⼼模式均为前沿 AI 平台授权  + 基于药企专有数据的定制模型开
发。
简单⽽⾔ Chai Discovery 不做管线，⽽是将⾃⼰定位为⼀个平台提供商，将 AI 软件平台授权给诺
华、辉瑞、礼来等⼤型药企使⽤。
这种平台型的商业模式，正在吸引⼀众资本的加⼊。
更让⼈意外的是 Chai 的体量。
这家公司⽬前只有将近40 名员⼯，⼏乎全是研究⼈员和科学家，甚⾄没有销售团队，只靠⼀个

⼩型 BD 团队对接药企。
在 Chai 看来，这恰恰说明了问题。
药企签约不是因为 AI 炒作，⽽是因为看到了真实的结果和价值，这样的交易不是每天去拜访药企
研发团队就能谈下来的，它需要能真正推动变⾰的技术。
不仅如此， Chai 还在挑战⼀个⾏业⾥的⽼共识。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
71 收藏 分享到 :
⻓期以来，向药企卖软件被认为是⼀⻔做得成、但天花板很低的⽣意。药企愿意为软件掏的钱
有限，真正的⼤钱，来⾃在药物项⽬⾥占有⼀部分经济权益。
但公司联合创始⼈ Meier 认为，这⼀次不⼀样了， AI 在药物发现中创造的价值已经远超以往，这
会颠覆很多关于商业模式的固有认知。
⽽且需求远没有⻅顶。早在今年 6 ⽉， Chai 就透露正在和超过 15 家药企谈判，并计划年内签下更
多合作。
同样卖模型，为什么国内药企很难买账？
⽽这条路，恰恰是国内 AI 制药最难复制的⼀条。
过去⼏年中国创新药最赚钱的⽅式，是做出⼀个分⼦，再通过 license-out 或 NewCo 卖给海外药
企，所以它们更愿意为管线付钱，⽽不是为软件付费。
再加上国内整体的软件付费习惯本就薄弱，按年订阅⼀个 AI 平台，在⼤多数药企的采购逻辑⾥很

难找到位置。
Chai 最锋利的能⼒是抗体从头设计，价值最⼤的场景是难成药靶点和 first-in-class 项⽬。
⽽国内创新药的主流打法仍以 fast-follow 和 me-better 为主，靶点⼤多已被验证，传统的筛选和优
化流程早已⾜够成熟， AI 从头设计带来的边际价值⾃然有限。
研发预算的量级差距也摆在那⾥，头部 MNC ⼀年的研发投⼊以百亿美元计，国内头部药企是⼏
⼗亿⼈⺠币量级，能拿出来试新⼯具的空间要⼩得多。
再加上融资环境收紧，平台型公司往往被逼着转向⾃建管线或项⽬制服务。⾄于把客⼾做到海
外 MNC ，⼜会撞上⽇益收紧的地缘政治与数据合规审查。
并且随着跨国药企对 AI 企业越来越重视，对合作⽅也会越来越挑剔。国内药企需要有过硬的突破
性成果，以及经过药企湿实验验证的硬证据。
当然，并不是没有国内公司尝试过这条路。晶泰科技、百图⽣科等公司都曾与海外⼤药企签下
平台或研发合作。
但⼀家中国 AI 公司想拿到 MNC 核⼼管线的数据、建⽴ Chai 那样的深度绑定，难度要⼤得多。

所以，真正的变量既在 AI 公司⾝上，⽽在国内药企⾝上。
当国内头部药企开始⼤规模做突破式创新，开始为更难的靶点发愁时， AI 从头设计才会从锦上添
花变成刚需。
本⽂来源：智药局

Document 295: Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #labour #employment
Q3 Job Vacancies: another step lower
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
13:07 October 01 2026 Share
The number of job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August. The decline
was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr. In contrast, public
sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr.

### Page 2

Job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August, extending the previous quarter’s
decline. The May and February estimates were also revised slightly lower. As a result, vacancies are
now 1.4% below their level a year earlier. The result was at odds with partial indicators, which all
pointed to a rise in the quarter.

The decline was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr to be 1.5%yr lower.
In contrast, public sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr, marking the strongest quarterly increase in four years
and partially reversing the sharp 7.5%qtr decline recorded in the May quarter. Public sector vacancies
were flat over the year.

Job vacancies have largely moved sideways since early 2025, though a resumption of the broader
downtrend now appears increasingly likely. Labour demand has held up reasonably well, though not
sufficiently to match growth in labour supply, resulting in a gradual easing in labour market conditions
(see here). Today’s result is also consistent with our view that this process will continue into early 2027
as the cumulative effects of high inflation and higher interest rates continue to weigh on activity and
hiring demand.

### Page 3

The vacancy-to-unemployment ratio, a key measure of labour market tightness closely monitored by
the RBA, declined from 0.48 to 0.45. The fall was the sharpest since early 2025, reflecting both a
decline in vacancies and a 6.8%qtr increase in the number of unemployed people over the quarter,
largely owing to the continued strength in labour supply. The ratio remains well above its long-run
average of around 0.2.

Given the strong growth in labour supply, the Beveridge curve provides a useful framework for
assessing recent labour market developments. By comparing vacancies and unemployment as shares
of the labour force, it controls for growth in the overall size of the labour market. Between 2022 and
2024, the labour market moved down the steep portion of the Beveridge curve, with substantial
declines in vacancies occurring alongside only modest increases in unemployment. While labour
market tightness has continued to unwind, Australia remains in this region of the curve, suggesting that
further rebalancing between labour demand and supply can occur through lower vacancies without a
material rise in unemployment.

### Page 4

All statistics mentioned below are not seasonally adjusted and hence may not add up to the seasonally
adjusted statistics referenced above.

By industry, job vacancies declined in eight of the 18 industries over the three months to August. The
largest falls were recorded in wholesale trade (–18.2%qtr), professional, scientific & technical services
(–17.4%qtr), and information media & telecommunications (–16.7%qtr). Consumer-facing sectors were
mixed, with retail trade (24.6%) and arts & recreation services (10.3%) recording strong increases,
while accommodation & food services edged 0.9% lower. Only three industries have seen vacancies
fall below their pre-pandemic levels: administrative & support services (–24.3%qtr), information media
& telecommunications (–9.1%qtr), and financial & insurance services (–7.3%qtr).

Overall market sector vacancies were flat in the quarter, meanwhile the non-market sector saw a 2.9%
drop, owing to declines in education & training (–8.2%qtr) and health care & social assistance (–
4.2%qtr). For detailed insights on industry condition, see Q2 Labour Account and the Westpac
Business Signal.

The Trump-Xi meeting in Washington met our key expectation: It delivered a subdued-yet-constructive summit resulting in a modest number of concrete deliverables that will minimise the near-term risks of abrupt
decoupling, a security crisis, or a disruptive trade war. This was the best plausible outcome for markets, given the structural nature of China-US competition. The brief two-month extension of the Busan agreement until

10 Jan 2027 will further delay new tariffs and export controls on both sides. It will also increase pressure to deliver a broader trade and investment package, and further trade-truce extensions, by the end of the year. We
anticipate more talks between US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng in Q4 as both sides seek a larger deal.
For China, stability with Washington will likely remain a priority as it manages rising trade tensions with the EU and prepares for top leadership reshuffles at its 2027 Party Congress. Combined with shifting US public

perceptions of China, these factors could create near-term space for more dealmaking on the sidelines of the upcoming APEC (18-19 Nov) and G20 (14-15 Dec) summits. As our surveys below show, US voters are now
more open to increased trade and investment with China, even at the cost of geopolitical concessions, while concerns over the cost of living have intensified. Both trends are now at their highest levels since April.
Nevertheless, political guardrails are not a panacea for a relationship that remains characterized by mistrust and competing national security priorities. As the next slide will show, the outlook for China-US relations

becomes more complex beyond this near-term horizon….

An analysis of the geopolitical calendar reveals a complex slate of tariffs, import curbs, export controls, and sensitive pol itical events. Even as leader-level diplomacy has reduced the risk of a near-term breakdown, it
has done little to stem the tide of new bilateral economic restrictions. These have proliferated during 2026 to include new e xport controls, sanctions, and tariffs (see list), with significant implications for high-growth

industries including drones, AI, electric vehicles, and robotics.
Competition is unlikely to slow, and events could repeatedly test the fragile trade truce. Q4 2026 will see further China-US dialogue and China-EU trade talks. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting increased
European trade pressure, Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. But the coming months will also bring challenges in the form of new US overcapacity tariffs and polysilicon levies, while next year will usher

in curbs on critical minerals and additional semiconductor tariffs. The run-up to the 10 Jan Busan truce expiry could also bring new economic restrictions as both sides seek negotiating leverage. Both countries will also
hold major political events (China’s Party Congress in 2027 and US elections in 2028), with potential implications for bilateral ties, while Taiwan’s Jan 2028 election will be another key watchpoint.
But these are just the events whose outcomes and impacts we can estimate in advance. Understanding probable China-US developments that are not on the calendar is equally important for investors. We should

assume that competitive dynamics across myriad policy issues will drive other developments and policy changes, often announce d with little or no advance notice. We examine some of the key issues in the next slide.

### Page 4

Miha Hribernik | Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties |  September 2026
Unless consistently managed, structural competition could move markets and test stability through the end of the
decade and beyond. We highlight five watchpoints for markets:
1. The AI race: Dialogue is set to strengthen AI safety guardrails, but national and economic security imperatives
mean it is unlikely to significantly slow the China-US AI race, much less meaningfully reverse existing restrictions.

Any attempts to pace AI development would touch on questions of tech restrictions, including Beijing’s perceptions
of US technological containment, which could increase amid potential restrictions on remote access to AI chips and
Chinese data centre components.
2. Tariff pressures: Trade tensions have subsided given a lower US trade deficit and the new Board of Trade, which is
set to reduce tariffs on US$60 billion of non-sensitive goods. But significant differences persist: Washington plans

to unveil new polysilicon and semiconductor tariffs, as well as overcapacity levies on 16 economies (including
China). The US is also seeking to restrict Chinese inputs in North American supply chains and target alleged
transshipment, while Beijing shores up its supply chain dominance in areas of US import dependence, such as
advanced drone components and processed critical minerals – which continue to be a source of friction.
3. Regulatory toolkits: The wider tech sector remains central to expanding export controls and restrictions. China is

strengthening its toolkit. Its 2026 codification of anti-derisking regulations strengthens Beijing’s ability to take
action over foreign sanctions or export controls. Meanwhile, its outbound investment regulations, effective 1 Jul,
cover technology, data, talent and capital. In the US, Congress is drafting legislation to change outbound
investment reporting requirements for biotech and biopharma, restrict government procurement of humanoid
robots and solar equipment, and ban the use of China-linked connected vehicles on military installations.

4. Geopolitical stress-tests: The war in Iran is unlikely to derail relations in the near term, but the conflict has strained
bilateral ties. Any potential broadening of the conflict in scale and intensity, or a further expansion of US sanctions
that impact Chinese interests, could test guardrails. Additionally, while the probability of conflict in the Taiwan
Strait remains low, differences over Taiwan could remain unresolved across successive administrations in both

Washington and Beijing into the 2030s and beyond.
5. Political anchors: Two domestic events will shape relations into the 2030s. Beijing’s priority is stability ahead of the
late 2027 Party Congress. For China, dialogue can help reduce the risk of costly escalation, potentially slow the
pace of new US economic restrictions, and buy time for efforts to strengthen self-reliance in strategic sectors such
as semiconductors (where China is aiming for 80% self-sufficiency by 2030).

In the US, a durable bipartisan consensus on China could make visible concessions to Beijing increasingly difficult
ahead of the 2028 presidential election. Recent bipartisan criticism of the Trump administration’s decision to allow
limited Nvidia H200 chip sales to China reflects the political constraints facing a transactional approach to China,
raising the prospect that guardrails will strain closer to election day.

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori
